## The Electrical Double Layer and the Theory of Electrocapillarity by David C. Grahame

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by David C. Grahame](chemistry-author.md#work-by-david-c-grahame)

Chemical Reviews 41(3), 441–501. Publisher: [https://doi.org/10.1021/cr60130a002](https://doi.org/10.1021/cr60130a002)

Electrochemical Society primary-paper archive: [https://knowledge.electrochem.org/estir/hist/hist-66-Grahame.pdf](https://knowledge.electrochem.org/estir/hist/hist-66-Grahame.pdf)

Section VI, printed pp. 470–474, derives the diffuse-layer theory from Poisson and Boltzmann equations. Equations (43)–(48), printed p. 474, distinguish integrated charge, integral [capacitance](electromagnetism.md#capacitance) and differential [capacitance](electromagnetism.md#capacitance). The archived PDF includes two preliminary pages before printed p. 441.

## Understanding the Extraordinary Flexibility of Polydimethylsiloxane through Single-Molecule Mechanics by Song Lu and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Song Lu](chemistry.md#work-by-song-lu)

ACS Materials Letters 4, 329–335.

Publisher: [https://doi.org/10.1021/acsmaterialslett.1c00655](https://doi.org/10.1021/acsmaterialslett.1c00655)

The primary-paper abstract describes single-chain atomic-force measurements and calculations supporting the flexibility of [polydimethylsiloxane](polymer.md#polydimethylsiloxane). It is a modern material-property reference, not a source supplied with the historical exam.

## Properties of some rodlike polymers in solution by C.-P. Wong and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by C.-P. Wong](chemistry.md#work-by-c-p-wong)

Journal of [polymer](polymer.md) Science: [polymer](polymer.md) Symposia 65, 173–192.

Publisher: [https://doi.org/10.1002/polc.5070650116](https://doi.org/10.1002/polc.5070650116)

The primary-paper abstract reports light-scattering and viscometry characterization of relatively stiff synthetic [polymers](polymer.md) in solution.

## Thermodynamics of High Polymer Solutions by Paul J. Flory

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Paul J. Flory](chemistry.md#work-by-paul-j-flory)

Journal of Chemical Physics 10, 51–61. [https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1723621](https://doi.org/10.1063/1.1723621)

Primary-paper scan: [https://www.appliedpolymertechnology.org/uploads/1/0/8/8/108867241/jchemphys_10_51.pdf](https://www.appliedpolymertechnology.org/uploads/1/0/8/8/108867241/jchemphys_10_51.pdf)

The lattice model treats connected [polymer](polymer.md) segments and derives the nonideal combinatorial [entropy](entropy.md) before adding the energetic mixing contribution.

## Definition and classification of colloids by International Union of Pure and Applied Chemistry

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)

[https://list.iupac.org/reports/2001/colloid_2001/manual_of_s_and_t/node33.html](https://list.iupac.org/reports/2001/colloid_2001/manual_of_s_and_t/node33.html)

The official terminology section defines the [colloidal](mixture.md#colloid) range as roughly $1\,\mathrm{nm}$ to $1\,\mathrm{\mu m}$ in at least one dimension and classifies systems by dispersed and continuous constituents.

## Cylindrical Primary Lithium Handbook and Application Manual: Lithium Iron Disulfide by Energizer

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Energizer](engineering.md#work-by-energizer)

Manufacturer technical handbook, Form 12004-A, 17 pages in the inspected copy. Printed pp.3–4 describe metallic-lithium construction and two-stage iron-disulfide conversion discharge; printed p.16 identifies the product as nonrechargeable. A web upload directory is not treated as the original publication date.

## The Role of Lead in Suppressing Passivation of High Silver-Containing Copper Anodes During Electrorefining by Labone L. Godirilwe and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Labone L. Godirilwe](chemistry-author.md#work-by-labone-l-godirilwe)

International Journal of the Society of Materials Engineering for Resources 25(1), 137–144; the inspected issue header is April 2022, whereas the PDF also carries a 2021 copyright and 2020 acceptance. Cite the issue date rather than silently interpreting acceptance as publication. Sections 2–3 specify controlled Cu–Ag–Pb [anodes](electrochemistry.md#anode), sulfate [electrolyte](electrochemistry.md#electrolyte), [electrorefining](electrochemistry.md#electrorefining) and SEM/EDS/XRD characterization of slime.

[https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/ijsmer/25/1/25_137/_pdf](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/ijsmer/25/1/25_137/_pdf)

## Theory, Production and Mechanism of Formation of Monodispersed Hydrosols by Victor K. La Mer and Robert H. Dinegar

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Robert H. Dinegar](chemistry-author.md#work-by-robert-h-dinegar), [Work by Victor K. La Mer](chemistry-author.md#work-by-victor-k-la-mer)

Journal of the American Chemical Society 72(11), 4847–4854 (1950), DOI 10.1021/ja01167a001. The publisher title/issue metadata and indexed original first-page discussion were inspected; direct publisher full-text retrieval was unavailable. The citation supports the historical sulfur-hydrosol [nucleation](critical-phenomenon.md#nucleation)/growth model, not a claim that it has been demonstrated for all nanoparticle syntheses.

[https://pubs.acs.org/jacsat/article/72/11/4847/515295/Theory-Production-and-Mechanism-of-Formation-of](https://pubs.acs.org/jacsat/article/72/11/4847/515295/Theory-Production-and-Mechanism-of-Formation-of)

[https://doi.org/10.1021/ja01167a001](https://doi.org/10.1021/ja01167a001)

<h2 id="the-promoter-role-of-sulfur-in-carbon-nanotube-growth-by-balazs-orban-and-tibor-holtzl">The promoter role of sulfur in carbon nanotube growth by Balázs Orbán and Tibor Höltzl</h2>

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Balázs Orbán](chemistry.md#work-by-balazs-orban), [Work by Tibor Höltzl](chemistry.md#work-by-tibor-holtzl)

[https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2022/dt/d2dt00355d](https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2022/dt/d2dt00355d)

Dalton Transactions 51, 9256–9264, DOI 10.1039/D2DT00355D. First-principles study finds weaker iron-carbon interaction with [sulfur](chemical-substance.md#sulfur). Later mechanistic corroboration, not a student answer set for this examination.

## Molecular-Level Control over Surface Order in Self-Assembled Monolayer Films of Thiols on Gold by Colin D. Bain and George M. Whitesides

Entry S05501, citing the 1972 [colloid](mixture.md#colloid) terminology manual, page 610. The definition establishes counterion-valence sensitivity; the sixth-power estimate additionally assumes the high-potential DLVO model.

Full title: O2 A-band line parameters to support atmospheric remote sensing. Part II: The rare isotopologues. Section 3, manuscript page 4, distinguishes rotational $N$ from total $J$ and states the odd-$N$ nuclear-spin statistical restriction for the dominant oxygen isotopologues.

Journal of the Chemical Society, Dalton Transactions, 196–200. The published title uses bracketed formulas and superscript [oxidation states](chemical-reaction.md#oxidation-state). ## New Carbaalanes Containing Clusters of Aluminum and Carbon Atoms by Werner Uhl and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Werner Uhl](chemistry-author.md#work-by-werner-uhl)

[https://doi.org/10.1021/om000244s](https://doi.org/10.1021/om000244s)

Primary study in Organometallics, volume 19, pages 4536–4543. Its abstract identifies the open [carbaalane](chemistry.md#carbaalane) with two $C=CHPh$ groups and reports their [hydroalumination](chemical-reaction.md#hydroalumination) during conversion to a capped aluminium cube. This establishes that the unsaturated groups are real, but the abstract alone does not establish which [electron counting](chemistry.md#electron-counting) convention the examination intended.

## Total synthesis of (±)-tirandamycin A by Philip DeShong and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Philip DeShong](chemistry-author.md#work-by-philip-deshong)

[https://doi.org/10.1021/ja00304a031](https://doi.org/10.1021/ja00304a031)

Primary synthetic study. Pages 5220–5221, Schemes II–III, report aqueous [hydrogen fluoride](electronegativity.md#hydrogen-fluoride) conversion of related pyranones into a fused [lactone](chemical-substance.md#lactone). The accompanying discussion qualifies the relative [stereochemistry](stereochemistry.md) as a mechanistic assignment rather than an unambiguous assignment from the reported [NMR spectroscopy](nuclear-magnetic-resonance.md).

## Compilation of Thermal Properties of Hydrogen in Its Various Isotopic and Ortho-Para Modifications by Harold W. Woolley and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Harold W. Woolley](chemistry.md#work-by-harold-w-woolley)

[https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/jres/041/5/v41.n05.a03.pdf](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/jres/041/5/v41.n05.a03.pdf)

Journal of Research of the National Bureau of Standards 41, 379–475 (1948), RP1932. DOI: [https://doi.org/10.6028/jres.041.037](https://doi.org/10.6028/jres.041.037)

Section III.2 and table 12 give ortho–para equilibrium composition and compare state sums with spectral and thermal evidence. The compilation distinguishes fixed-composition [normal hydrogen](molecule.md#normal-hydrogen) from equilibrium [spin isomers of hydrogen](molecule.md#spin-isomers-of-hydrogen) mixtures. It supplies institutional thermodynamic data and theory, rather than answers to a particular examination.

## Hydrogen iodide: Constants of diatomic molecules by Klaus P. Huber and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Gerhard H. Herzberg](chemistry-author.md#work-by-gerhard-h-herzberg), [Work by Klaus P. Huber](chemistry-author.md#work-by-klaus-p-huber)

By [Klaus P. Huber](chemistry-author.md#klaus-p-huber) and [Gerhard H. Herzberg](chemistry-author.md#gerhard-h-herzberg). NIST Chemistry WebBook compilation of diatomic constants.

[https://webbook.nist.gov/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C10034852&Mask=1000](https://webbook.nist.gov/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C10034852&Mask=1000)

Cited row: ground $X\,{}^1\Sigma^+$ state of [hydrogen iodide](chemical-substance.md#hydrogen-iodide), $\omega_e=2309.014\ \mathrm{cm^{-1}}$ and $\omega_ex_e=39.6435\ \mathrm{cm^{-1}}$. Higher anharmonic coefficients are also tabulated, so the two-constant [Morse oscillator](quantum-system.md#morse-oscillator) extrapolation should not be confused with a measurement of the actual dissociation threshold.

## Hydrogen bromide: Constants of diatomic molecules by Klaus P. Huber and others

↑ **Parent:** [Chemistry bibliography](chemistry-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Gerhard H. Herzberg](chemistry-author.md#work-by-gerhard-h-herzberg), [Work by Klaus P. Huber](chemistry-author.md#work-by-klaus-p-huber)

By [Klaus P. Huber](chemistry-author.md#klaus-p-huber) and [Gerhard H. Herzberg](chemistry-author.md#gerhard-h-herzberg). NIST Chemistry WebBook compilation of diatomic constants.

[https://webbook.nist.gov/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C10035106&Mask=1000](https://webbook.nist.gov/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C10035106&Mask=1000)

Cited row: ground $X\,{}^1\Sigma^+$ state of [hydrogen bromide](chemical-substance.md#hydrogen-bromide), $\omega_e=2648.975\ \mathrm{cm^{-1}}$ and $\omega_ex_e=45.2175\ \mathrm{cm^{-1}}$. The isotope designation in this table is H81Br. These constants characterize low vibrational levels; an exact global [Morse potential](quantum-system.md#morse-potential) is a further modelling assumption.

Bull and others</h2>

Journal of the American Chemical Society 118, 2359–2365. Radiolabel recovery, denaturation, purification and spectroscopy establish the cofactor adduct. Figure 7 compares [enolate](chemical-substance.md#enolate) protonation with cofactor trapping. Slow dissociation/breakdown motivates a timescale-qualified description of inhibition.

Nature Communications 11, 5430. The NADP–dihydrofinasteride structure, mutant assays and modeled [testosterone](chemical-substance.md#testosterone) placement provide complementary evidence; a trapped inhibitor is not a directly observed [testosterone](chemical-substance.md#testosterone) transition state.

Journal of the Chemical Society, Dalton Transactions, 585–591. Kinetics with aromatic amines and methanol trapping support a base-promoted elimination/addition route under stated conditions; direct substitution remains important in other regimes.

Inorganic Chemistry 50, 8937–8945. Products prepared under strict anaerobic conditions were studied by crystallography and multinuclear variable-temperature NMR. These are ambient-temperature adduct observations, not direct identification of a high-temperature polymerization intermediate.
